**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/iwasawa-algebra-logarithm` · construction.

Let R = Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)} and J_R the Jacobson radical of the local ring R[Ḡ]^τ. Then J_R/pR[Ḡ]^τ is nilpotent (Lemma 65). For every ideal I ⊆ J_R, Log(1 + x) = Σ_{i≥1}(−1)^{i+1}x^i/i (x ∈ I) induces a unique homomorphism log_I : K₁(R[Ḡ]^τ, I) → (I/[R[Ḡ]^τ, I]) ⊗_{ℤ_p} ℚ_p; if I ⊆ ξR[Ḡ]^τ for a central ξ with ξ^p ∈ pξR[Ḡ]^τ, it lands in I/[R[Ḡ]^τ, I], and it is an isomorphism if moreover I^p ⊆ pIJ_R (Proposition 67). For Λ_𝒪(G), with G_n = G/Z^{p^n} and F = Frac 𝒪, the logarithms log : K₁(𝒪[G_n]) → F[Conj G_n] of Oliver kill torsion and pass to the limit, log : K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → lim_n F[Conj G_n] = F⟦Z⟧[Conj Ḡ]^τ.

**Hypotheses.**

- G is a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with a closed normal H, G/H ≅ Γ ≅ ℤ_p, a fixed lift G = H ⋊ Γ, Z = Γ^{p^e} open in Γ and acting trivially on H (so Z is central), Ḡ = G/Z, and U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ; p is odd; 𝒪 is the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Finite products are handled componentwise, taking products of the entire groups and exact sequences, including separate G^ab factors.
- G pro-p, so R[Ḡ]^τ is local.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 65: over k = 𝒪/p there is an exact sequence 0 → J_R/p → Q(k⟦G⟧) → Q(k⟦Γ⟧) → 0; the kernel N of k⟦G⟧ → k⟦Γ⟧ is k⟦G⟧I_H with I_H nilpotent (H is a finite p-group), and by Lemma 37 the denominators can be taken central.
2. Lemma 66 (Oliver's Lemma 2.7 for twisted Iwasawa algebras): R[Ḡ]^τ is J_R-adically complete, so Log converges on 1 + I into I[1/p] (into I under the ξ-hypothesis, since then I^n ⊆ nI); Log(uv) ≡ Log u + Log v modulo U(I) = Σ_{m≥0,n≥1}(1/(m+n))[I^m, I^n] by summing the coefficients of cyclically equivalent words; under I^p ⊆ pIJ_R, Exp converges and inverts Log, and Exp([R[Ḡ]^τ, I]) ⊆ E(R[Ḡ]^τ, I) by Vaserstein's identity Exp(rx)Exp(xr)⁻¹ ∈ E(R[Ḡ]^τ, I) and successive approximation through D_k = ⟨rx − xr : rx, xr ∈ IJ_R^k⟩.
3. Proposition 67 (Oliver's Theorem 2.8): apply Lemma 66 to M_n(I) ⊆ M_n(R[Ḡ]^τ), compose with the trace Tr_n, note L_n([r, u]) = Tr_n(rLog(u)r⁻¹) − Tr_n(Log u) = 0, and pass to K₁(R[Ḡ]^τ, I) = GL(R[Ḡ]^τ, I)/[GL(R[Ḡ]^τ), GL(R[Ḡ]^τ, I)].
4. The logarithm on K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) is the finite-level logarithm in the limit. At finite level it kills the torsion Det(G_n) by ClassicalArithmeticCompletion §7.73; the rest of Oliver's finite-level input is obligation 5.

**API.**

- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.logRel` (constructor): log_I : K₁(R[Ḡ]^τ, I) → (I/[R[Ḡ]^τ, I]) ⊗ ℚ_p.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.logRel_integral` (characterisation): Under the ξ-hypothesis, log_I lands in I/[R[Ḡ]^τ, I].
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.logRel_bijective` (equivalence): log_I is an isomorphism when moreover I^p ⊆ pIJ_R.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.log_mul_mod_commutators` (relation): Log(uv) ≡ Log u + Log v mod [R[Ḡ]^τ[1/p], I[1/p]] (Lemma 66).
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.logK1Prime` (constructor): log : K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → F⟦Z⟧[Conj Ḡ]^τ, the limit of the finite-level logarithms.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.log_natural` (functoriality): log commutes with the maps induced by group homomorphisms (used in Lemma 81).

**Unit tests.**

- `commutative_case`: Ḡ = 1: [R, I] = 0 and log_I(1 + x) = log(1 + x) is the usual p-adic logarithm.
- `jacobson_nilpotent`: G = ℤ_p × C_p, k = 𝔽_p: J_R/p is generated by g − 1, and (g − 1)^p = g^p − 1 = 0 in k[C_p].
- `torsion_killed`: log is not injective: for G = ℤ_p × C_p the class of g has order p in K′₁(Λ(G)), so log(g) = 0 although g ≠ 1.
- `bijectivity_hypothesis`: The analogue over ℤ₂ with I = 2ℤ₂ fails I^p ⊆ pIJ_R (4ℤ₂ ⊄ 8ℤ₂), and log(1 + 2ℤ₂) = 4ℤ₂ ≠ 2ℤ₂; for odd p and I = pℤ_p the hypothesis holds and log : 1 + pℤ_p ≅ pℤ_p.

**Check.**

- Kakde notes (Remark 68) that for R = Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)} it is unclear whether log_{J_R} extends canonically to K₁(R[Ḡ]^τ); only log_{J_R} is used.

**Uses.**

- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/theta-beta-relation: Lemmas 76, 77 and 81
- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/integral-logarithm: L = log − (ϕ/p)log

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/twisted-group-ring-presentation](#ne6-twisted-group-ring-presentation), `KTheoryLowDegrees:U.5/relative-K1`, `KTheoryLowDegrees:U.5/relative-elementary-subgroup`, `KTheoryLowDegrees:U.5/relative-K1-radical-ideal`, `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/continuation/group-log-commutator-isomorphism`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.4, Proposition 67, p. 46 (arXiv v3): Proposition 67 with Lemmas 65–66 (pp. 42–47), Remark 68, and the limit of finite-level logarithms (p. 47).

<a id="ne6-integral-logarithm"></a>

### NE.6.12. The integral logarithm on K′₁ of Iwasawa algebras

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/integral-logarithm` · construction.

Let ϕ be the endomorphism of R (Frobenius on 𝒪, p-power map on Z), extended to R[Conj Ḡ]^τ by [g] ↦ [g^p] (Definition 69). Oliver–Taylor's integral logarithm L = log − (ϕ/p)log : K′₁(𝒪[G_n]) → 𝒪[Conj G_n] sits in an exact sequence 1 → μ(𝒪) × G_n^ab → K′₁(𝒪[G_n]) → 𝒪[Conj G_n] → G_n^ab → 1 with ω(Σ a_g g) = ∏ g^{tr_{𝒪/ℤ_p}(a_g)} (Oliver, Theorem 6.6); its inverse limit is L : K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ with 1 → μ(𝒪) × G^ab → K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ → G^ab → 1 exact (Definition 70). On the completed localisation, with J = ker(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S → Λ̂_𝒪(Γ)_{(p)}) and the splitting Γ ↪ G, every x ∈ K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S) is x = uy with u in the image of K₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S, J) and y ∈ K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(Γ)_{(p)}) (Lemma 71); y^p/ϕ(y) ≡ 1 mod p (Lemma 72); and L(uy) = log(u) − (ϕ/p)log(u) + (1/p)log(y^p/ϕ(y)) is a homomorphism K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S) → Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)}[Conj Ḡ]^τ, independent of the splitting (Definition 73, Proposition 74).

**Hypotheses.**

- G is a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with a closed normal H, G/H ≅ Γ ≅ ℤ_p, a fixed lift G = H ⋊ Γ, Z = Γ^{p^e} open in Γ and acting trivially on H (so Z is central), Ḡ = G/Z, and U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ; p is odd; 𝒪 is the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Finite products are handled componentwise, taking products of the entire groups and exact sequences, including separate G^ab factors.
- G pro-p; 𝒪 unramified, so (p) is the maximal ideal of 𝒪.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Definition 70, finite level. Read K′₁(O[G_n]) as the determinant image (NE.3/k1-prime). Write O[G_n]^×=O^×·(1+I) with I the augmentation ideal. ClassicalArithmeticCompletion Layer 7 supplies the integral logarithm with its character formula (§7.46), L(1+I)⊆pφ(I) and L(1+A)=pφ(A) on the commutator ideal A (§7.62), and ker(L/p on Det(1+I))=Det(G_n) (§7.73), which is G_n^ab because one-dimensional characters detect G_n^ab; its L/p is this node's L. On O^× this L is (1−F/p)log, with kernel μ(O) and image O·[1], because log(O^×)=pO and F−p is bijective on unramified O. Since O·[1]∩φ(I)=0, the kernel is μ(O)×G_n^ab and the image contains O·[1]+φ(A). ω is onto because the trace of O onto Z_p is onto. The abelian image L(1+I)=I∩ker ω for G_n^ab, with ω∘L=1, is Oliver Theorem 6.6's abelian case (obligation 5).
2. Definition 70, limit: the sequences (9) for n ≥ 0 are compatible sequences of profinite groups, so the inverse limit is exact.
3. Lemma 71: Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S is local, so its units surject onto K′₁ and 1 + J onto K₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S, J); 1 → 1 + J → Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S^× → Λ̂_𝒪(Γ)_{(p)}^× → 1 splits by Γ ↪ G.
4. Lemma 72: modulo p, Λ̂_𝒪(Γ)_{(p)} is the Laurent series field 𝔽_q((X)), on which ϕ is X ↦ (1 + X)^p − 1 = X^p with Frobenius on 𝔽_q, so y^p = ϕ(y).
5. Integrality: L(1 − v) ≡ Σ_k (v^{pk} − ϕ(v^k))/(pk) modulo the integral elements, and pk ∣ v^{pk} − ϕ(v^k) follows from p^n ∣ v^{p^n} − ϕ(v^{p^{n−1}}), proved by grouping the monomials of v^{p^n} into orbits under cyclic permutation (legitimate because τ is symmetric) and p^t ∣ r̂^{p^t} − ϕ(r̂^{p^{t−1}}).
6. Independence of the splitting: the finitely many splittings agree on an open A ≤ G; L is independent on the images of K₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S, J_R) and K₁(Λ̂_𝒪(A)_S), whose product has torsion cokernel in K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S), and the target is torsion free.

**API.**

- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.frobeniusPhi` (constructor): ϕ on R and on R[Conj Ḡ]^τ, [g] ↦ [g^p].
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.integralLog` (constructor): L : K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.omegaMap` (constructor): ω : Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ → G^ab.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.integralLog_exact` (characterisation): 1 → μ(𝒪) × G^ab → K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ → G^ab → 1 is exact.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.integralLogCompleted` (constructor): L : K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S) → Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)}[Conj Ḡ]^τ (Definition 73).
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.integralLogCompleted_indep` (characterisation): Independent of the splitting Γ ↪ G (Proposition 74).
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.integralLog_compat` (compatibility): The completed L restricts to L on the image of K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)).

**Unit tests.**

- `scalar_integrality`: 𝒪 = ℤ_p and v scalar: p^n ∣ v^{p^n} − v^{p^{n−1}}; for p = 3, n = 3 checked on ℤ/27.
- `frobenius_mod_p`: Modulo p the completed localization of O[[Γ]] at (p) is F_q((T)), where ϕ(T)=T^p and coefficients undergo Frobenius. In particular T is invertible; F_q[[T]] would be the wrong residue ring.
- `torsion_kernel`: For G=Γ×C_p, L vanishes on G^ab. In particular L(γ)=0 although γ has infinite order. The whole kernel is not the torsion-unit subgroup.
- `not_log`: L ≠ log: for Ḡ = 1, 𝒪 = ℤ_p and x = 1 + p (p odd), L(x) = (1 − 1/p)log(1 + p) ≠ log(1 + p), because ϕ is trivial on ℤ_p.

**Check.**

- Kakde leaves the kernel and cokernel of L on K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S) open (Remark 75, citing Ritter–Weiss); only the homomorphism is planned.

**Uses.**

- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/theta-beta-relation: β^G_P(L(x)) through θ^G(x)
- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/phi-integral-log-sequence: the top row of the five-lemma ladder
- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems: the exact sequence of Definition 70

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/iwasawa-algebra-logarithm](#ne6-iwasawa-algebra-logarithm), [NE.6/twisted-group-ring-presentation](#ne6-twisted-group-ring-presentation), [NE.3/k1-prime](#ne3-k1-prime), `mathlib:ZMod.expand_card`, `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/continuation/integral-group-log`, `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/continuation/group-log-integrality`, `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/continuation/group-log-commutator-isomorphism`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.5, Definition 70 and Proposition 74, pp. 48–49 (arXiv v3): Definitions 69–70 with the sequence (9) (p. 48), Lemmas 71–72, Definition 73 and Proposition 74 (pp. 48–50), Remark 75 (p. 51).

<a id="ne6-theta-beta-relation"></a>

### NE.6.13. The additive and multiplicative sides: β^G(L(x)) through θ^G(x)

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/theta-beta-relation` · theorem.

Define v^G_P on ∏_C F⟦Z⟧[C^ab] by v^G_P((x_C)) = Σ_{P′} ([P′ : P′^p]/[P : P′^p])ϕ(x_{P′}) over cyclic P′ with P′^p ≤ P when P is not cyclic, and Σ_{P′ ≠ P′^p = P} [P′ : P′^p]ϕ(x_{P′}) when P is cyclic (Definition 78); and u^G_P on ∏_C Λ_𝒪(U_C^ab)^× by u^G_P((x_C)) = ∏_{P′^p ≤ P} ϕ(x_{P′})^{|P′|} for noncyclic P and ∏_{P′ ≠ P′^p = P} ϕ(x_{P′}) for cyclic P, over cyclic P′ (Definition 80). Then for x ∈ K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)): β^G_P(L(x)) = (1/(p²|P|))log(α_P(θ^G_P(x))^{p|P|}/u^G_P(α(θ^G(x)))) for noncyclic P; β^G_P(L(x)) = (1/p)log(α_P(θ^G_P(x))/u^G_P(α(θ^G(x)))) for nontrivial cyclic P; and β^G_{1}(L(x)) = (1/p)log(α_{1}(θ^G_{1}(x))/(ϕ(θ^G_{1}(x))u^G_{1}(α(θ^G(x))))) (Proposition 84); the same holds on K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S). The logarithms are defined: α_P(θ^G_P(x))^{p|P|} ≡ u^G_P(α(θ^G(x))) mod p for noncyclic P (Lemma 82), and the cyclic quotients are ≡ 1 mod p (Remark 83).

**Hypotheses.**

- G is a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with a closed normal H, G/H ≅ Γ ≅ ℤ_p, a fixed lift G = H ⋊ Γ, Z = Γ^{p^e} open in Γ and acting trivially on H (so Z is central), Ḡ = G/Z, and U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ; p is odd; 𝒪 is the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Finite products are handled componentwise, taking products of the entire groups and exact sequences, including separate G^ab factors.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 76: log ∘ θ^G_P = t^G_P ∘ log (Oliver, Theorem 6.8); in the completed case from log(u) = lim p^{−n}(u^{p^n} − 1) and θ̂^G_P(u) = lim (1 + t^G_P(u^{p^n} − 1))^{1/p^n}.
2. Lemma 77: log ∘ α_P = pη_P ∘ log for nontrivial cyclic P, since ω_P commutes with log and η_P = (1/p)(p − Σ_k ω_P^k).
3. Lemma 79: β^G_P ∘ ϕ = v^G_P ∘ β^G for P ≠ {1}, and β^G_{1} ∘ ϕ = (ϕ + v^G_{1}) ∘ β^G, by counting cosets.
4. Lemma 81: log ∘ u^G_P = |P|·v^G_P ∘ log for noncyclic P and (1/p)·v^G_P ∘ log for cyclic P, by naturality of log.
5. Lemma 82: modulo p, α_C(θ^G_C(x)) ≡ 1 for nontrivial cyclic C and θ^G_P(x)^{|P|} ≡ θ^G_{1}(x), the latter by Schneider–Venjakob, Proposition 2.3 (on K₁(𝔽_p⟦H⟧), the norm to an open normal N followed by the inclusion is x ↦ x^{[H:N]}).
6. Proposition 84 by chaining Lemmas 76, 77, 79 and 81. On the completed ring write x = uy (Lemma 71): u is handled identically; for y ∈ Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)}, θ^G_P(y) = y^{[Ḡ:P]}, α_P(θ^G_P(y)) = 1 for nontrivial cyclic P and β^G_P(L(y)) = [Ḡ : P]L(y) or 0; a general y has ϕ^r(y) ∈ Λ̂_𝒪(Z)_{(p)} and y^{p^r}/ϕ^r(y) ≡ 1 mod p, and the targets are torsion free.

**Check.**

- At Ḡ = 1 both sides are (1/p)log(x^p/ϕ(x)).

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/integral-logarithm](#ne6-integral-logarithm), [NE.6/iwasawa-algebra-logarithm](#ne6-iwasawa-algebra-logarithm), [NE.6/additive-map-beta](#ne6-additive-map-beta), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.6, Proposition 84, p. 56 (arXiv v3): Proposition 84 with Lemmas 76, 77, 79, 81, 82, Definitions 78, 80 and Remark 83 (pp. 51–58).
- [SCHNEIDER-VENJAKOB-2010](#source-schneider-venjakob-2010), Proposition 2.3 and proof, pp. 11–12 (arXiv v1): Proposition 2.3 continues: K₁(Ω(H)) → K₁(Ω(N)) → K₁(Ω(H)), the norm followed by the canonical map, is x ↦ x^{[H:N]}, with Ω = 𝔽_p⟦−⟧. This is the input to Kakde's Lemma 82.

<a id="ne6-phi-integral-log-sequence"></a>

### NE.6.14. The multiplicative sequence for Φ^G

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/phi-integral-log-sequence` · lemma.

θ^G(K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G))) ⊆ Φ^G and θ̂^G_S(K′₁(Λ̂_𝒪(G)_S)) ⊆ Φ̂^G_S (Lemma 85). The map μ(𝒪) × G^ab → ∏_P Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^×, (ζ,g)↦(ζ^[G:U_P]·ver^G_(U_P)(g))_P, with the scalar norm exponent and the group transfer in each component, is injective and commutes with θ^G (Lemma 86). There is a surjection ω : ψ^G → G^ab, ω_ψ=ω_A∘δ, using the additive inverse δ:ψ^G≅Λ_O(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ, with ω ∘ β^G = ω (Lemma 87). The map ℒ = (ℒ_P) : Φ^G → ψ^G, with ℒ_P((x_C)) given by the right-hand sides of Proposition 84 with x_C in place of θ^G_C(x), sits in an exact sequence 1 → μ(𝒪) × G^ab → Φ^G → ψ^G → G^ab → 1 (Lemma 88).

**Hypotheses.**

- G is a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with a closed normal H, G/H ≅ Γ ≅ ℤ_p, a fixed lift G = H ⋊ Γ, Z = Γ^{p^e} open in Γ and acting trivially on H (so Z is central), Ḡ = G/Z, and U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ; p is odd; 𝒪 is the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Finite products are handled componentwise, taking products of the entire groups and exact sequences, including separate G^ab factors.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 85, M1: the norm to U_P equals the norm to U_{P′} followed by nr^{P′}_P after abelianising, because the bases agree. M2: the norm is conjugation-equivariant.
2. Lemma 85, M3: write x = Σ x_k g^k over Λ_𝒪(U_P) for g ∈ U_{P′} ∖ U_P; the norm is the determinant of the twisted circulant matrix, whose terms not fixed by σ = conjugation by g cancel modulo T_{P,P′}, and Σ_h a_h^p ∏_k σ^k(h) ≡ Σ_h ϕ(a_h)∏_k σ^k(h) = ver^{P′}_P modulo T_{P,P′}.
3. Lemma 85, M4: by Proposition 84 and Lemma 60, pβ^G_P(L(x)) = log(α_P(θ^G_P(x))/u^G_P(α(θ^G(x)))) ∈ pT_P, and log : 1 + pT_P ≅ pT_P.
4. Lemma 89: if C^p ⊆ P′ but C^p ⊄ P with [P′ : P] = p, then nr^{P′}_P(ϕ(α_C(x_C))) = 1, by Lemma 50.
5. ℒ lands in ψ^G: A1 by cases (P not cyclic; P cyclic and P′ not, using α_P(ϕ(α_C(x_C))) = 1 unless C^p = P; P′ cyclic by Lemma 89) from M1; A2 and A3 from M2 and M4; integrality from Proposition 64.
6. Correct the first map to θ∘ι: the component of a scalar ζ is ζ^[G:U_P], since a scalar acts on a free module of that rank; the group component is the ordinary transfer. The top component identifies μ(O)×G^ab inside Λ_O(G^ab)^×, proving injectivity. The other end is ω_ψ=ω_Aδ and is surjective because ω_A is surjective and δ is the inverse additive isomorphism.
7. Kernel, cyclic components: log is injective on 1+pΛ_O(U_P), since p is odd. Descend from maximal nontrivial cyclic P in the defining ℒ equations to obtain α_P(x_P)=1 for every nontrivial cyclic P. At P=1 obtain x_1^p=ϕ(x_1), not α_1(x_1)=1. The integral logarithm for the abelian group Z therefore gives x_1∈μ(O)×Z. For successive nontrivial cyclic P, M1 and α_P=1 give x_P^p=x_(P^p), viewed by inclusion, hence x_P^{|P|}=x_1.
8. Kernel, finite-level input and limit: for each abelian A=U_P^ab and finite quotient A_n=A/image(Z^{p^n}), ClassicalArithmeticCompletion §7.73 (kernel Det(A_n) on 1+I) and the scalar computation in NE.6/integral-logarithm identify the kernel with μ(O)×A_n. Compatibility and compactness give ker L_A=μ(O)×A. Since the additive target of L_A is Z_p-torsion-free, Λ_O(A)^×/(μ(O)×A) is torsion-free. The cyclic power relation puts x_P in μ(O)×U_P. For noncyclic P its defining equation gives x_P^{p²|P|}=ϕ(x_1)^p after all nontrivial α_C have become 1, and gives x_P∈μ(O)×U_P^ab by the same torsion-free quotient argument. This uses the separately named finite integral-log supplier, not a torsion-unit assertion about completed group units.
9. Kernel, trace rigidity: along any index-p inclusion P≤P′, write x_P=ξh and x_(P′)=ζg. M3 compares ξh with ζ^p ver(g). In every finite quotient U_P^ab/image(Z^{p^n}), augmentation and distinct Teichmüller residues imply ξ=ζ^p. The difference of the two remaining basis units is invariant under P′/P. Its support has at most two elements, so odd p makes each element fixed individually; coefficients on fixed elements in an index-p trace are divisible by p. Thus the difference is zero. Passing through the finite quotients proves x_P=ζ^p ver(g) exactly. An index-p subgroup chain from Ḡ to P recovers θ∘ι of x_Ḡ, with the scalar exponent [Ḡ:P].
10. Cokernel, untruncated abelianization: let π_ab be the projection of the additive conjugacy module to Λ_O(G^ab). Prove π_abδℒ(x)=L_(G^ab)(x_Ḡ). If Ḡ is cyclic, M1 identifies x with θ(x_Ḡ), and δℒθ=δβL=L. In particular do not replace δ by the generator-truncated top β component.
11. Cokernel, noncyclic calculation: work in Q_p⊗Λ_O(G^ab), put m=|Ḡ|, A_C=log α_C(x_C) for cyclic C (A_1=p log x_1), and U=Σ_(C cyclic)|C|ϕ(A_C). Since a∈ψ and Ḡ is noncyclic, its top β component is untruncated and equals π_abδa. The additive inverse gives π_abδa=m⁻¹Σ_(C cyclic)|C|a_C. For a=ℒ(x), substitution yields (pΣ|C|A_C−U)/(p²m); the untruncated top component is log x_Ḡ−U/(p²m). Equality of these two expressions forces Σ|C|A_C=pm log x_Ḡ; applying ϕ gives U=pmϕ(log x_Ḡ). Thus π_abδℒ(x)=log x_Ḡ−ϕ(log x_Ḡ)/p. Here log denotes the rational logarithm of the preceding logarithm construction; no unsupported power-series logarithm of an arbitrary unit is introduced.
12. Exactness at ψ: ω_A factors through π_ab and ω_(G^ab)L_(G^ab)=1, so ω_Aδℒ=1. Conversely, if ω_Aδa=1, the integral-log exact sequence gives z∈K′₁(Λ_O(G)) with Lz=δa. Then ℒθz=βLz=a. Products of coefficient domains take products of this entire argument. The five-lemma ladder now uses proved corrected maps and this kernel/cokernel reconstruction.

**Check.**

- For p=3 and O unramified of degree 2, let ζ have Teichmüller order 8. A rank-3 norm sends ζ to ζ³≠ζ; the scalar embedding cannot be the identity in every component.
- For G=Γ, Z=Γ^p and a=z=γ^p, β_(C_p)(a)=0 while ω_A(a)=z. Recovering ω from only a_(C_p) is false; ω_Aδ retains it.
- The kernel contains γ of infinite order. For O=∏_i O_i take ∏_i(μ(O_i)×G^ab), not μ(O)×a single G^ab.
- At a noncyclic P, ker ℒ gives a power of x_P in μ(O)×U_P^ab; it need not make x_P torsion. The torsion-free quotient supplied by the integral logarithm is the inference required here.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi), [NE.6/theta-beta-relation](#ne6-theta-beta-relation), [NE.6/additive-theorem](#ne6-additive-theorem), [NE.6/integral-logarithm](#ne6-integral-logarithm), `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/continuation/group-log-commutator-isomorphism`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.7, Lemmas 85–89, pp.58–65 (arXiv v3): The corrected statement and kernel/cokernel reconstruction replace the defective scalar embedding and cokernel computation; E21–E22 retain the original-version findings.
- [SCHNEIDER-VENJAKOB-AFTER-KAKDE](#source-schneider-venjakob-after-kakde), §4, Lemmas 4.7–4.9, pp. 26–30: The abelianized logarithm, torsion-free quotient and trace-rigidity proof provide the replacement route. Their α_Z=x_Z^p/ϕ(x_Z) absorbs the extra central factor that Kakde writes separately; their untruncated top map must not be identified with cyclic β_Ḡ. Its noncyclic kernel proof drops the central factor and makes a false intermediate torsion claim (E37); the reconstruction above retains φ(x_1)^p and uses the torsion-free quotient instead.

<a id="ne6-main-algebraic-theorems"></a>

### NE.6.15. Kakde's congruence description of K′₁

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems` · theorem.

Let G be a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with G/H ≅ ℤ_p, Z ≤ G central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z, and 𝒪 the integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p or a finite direct sum of such rings. Then θ^G induces an isomorphism K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) ≅ Φ^G (Theorem 52); θ^G_S maps K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)_S) into Φ^G_S, and Φ^G_S ∩ ∏_{P≤Ḡ} Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^× = θ^G(K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G))) (Theorem 53).

**Hypotheses.**

- G is a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group with a closed normal H, G/H ≅ Γ ≅ ℤ_p, a fixed lift G = H ⋊ Γ, Z = Γ^{p^e} open in Γ and acting trivially on H (so Z is central), Ḡ = G/Z, and U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ; p is odd; 𝒪 is the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Finite products are handled componentwise, taking products of the entire groups and exact sequences, including separate G^ab factors.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduce to 𝒪 unramified over ℤ_p (Remark 54).
2. Theorem 52: the five lemma on the ladder μ(𝒪) × G^ab → K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → Λ_𝒪(Z)[Conj Ḡ]^τ → G^ab (integral-logarithm) over μ(𝒪) × G^ab → Φ^G → ψ^G → G^ab (phi-integral-log-sequence), with θ^G, β^G (an isomorphism by additive-theorem) and the identities as vertical maps; the squares commute by Lemmas 86–87 and ℒ ∘ θ^G = β^G ∘ L (theta-beta-relation).
3. Theorem 53: θ^G_S lands in Φ̂^G_S ∩ ∏Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^×_S = Φ^G_S (Lemma 85 on the completed ring), and Φ^G_S ∩ ∏Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^× = Φ^G, which is θ^G(K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G))) by Theorem 52.

**Check.**

- Source-qualified: G pro-p of dimension one and 𝒪 unramified. The general one-dimensional G reaches this case through reduction-to-p-elementary, with 𝒪 = ℤ_p[Δ] ≅ ⊕_χ 𝒪_χ.
- Checked on Ḡ = 1 and Ḡ = C₂ in congruence-group-phi's tests.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/phi-integral-log-sequence](#ne6-phi-integral-log-sequence), [NE.6/additive-theorem](#ne6-additive-theorem), [NE.6/theta-beta-relation](#ne6-theta-beta-relation), [NE.6/integral-logarithm](#ne6-integral-logarithm), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi), [NE.3/k1-prime](#ne3-k1-prime).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.2.3, Theorems 52–53, p. 36 (arXiv v3); proofs p. 65: Theorems 52 and 53 (p. 36) with their proofs (p. 65).

**Atlas planet:** Kakde's congruence description of K′₁.

<a id="ne6-k1-injects-localisation"></a>

### NE.6.16. K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) injects into K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)_S)

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/k1-injects-localisation` · theorem.

Let G be a compact p-adic Lie group with a quotient G/H ≅ ℤ_p, and 𝒪 the ring of integers of a finite unramified extension of ℚ_p. Then the map K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)_S) is injective (Corollary 90).

**Hypotheses.**

- G compact p-adic Lie with G/H ≅ ℤ_p; 𝒪 unramified.
- For the Kakde theorem route, p is odd, as in the source’s standing assumption. Toy algebra calculations at 2 do not extend the theorem to 2.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G pro-p of dimension one: θ^G_S composed with K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) → K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)_S) is θ^G followed by the injection ∏Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^× ↪ ∏Λ_𝒪(U_P^ab)^×_S, and θ^G is injective (Theorem 52).
2. G of dimension one: reduction-to-qp-elementary, l-elementary-case and reduction-to-p-elementary each carry the injectivity of K′₁ → K′₁ of the localisation from their subquotients to G; they end at Δ × G_p with 𝒪[Δ] ≅ ⊕_χ 𝒪_χ unramified, which is the pro-p case.
3. Arbitrary dimension: if x ↦ 1 in K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)_S), then for every open U ≤ H normal in G and pro-p its image in K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G/U)_S) is 1, hence its image in K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G/U)) is 1 by the dimension-one case, and x = 1 because K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G)) ≅ lim_U K′₁(Λ_𝒪(G/U)) (k1-prime, Corollary 20).

**Check.**

- The source prints the corollary without a proof (p. 65). The route above is reconstructed and flagged. It runs the §4 reductions with 𝒪 = ℤ_p, as printed; a larger unramified 𝒪 needs the same reductions over 𝒪, which Kakde does not write out.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems](#ne6-main-algebraic-theorems), [NE.6/reduction-to-qp-elementary](#ne6-reduction-to-qp-elementary), [NE.6/l-elementary-case](#ne6-l-elementary-case), [NE.6/reduction-to-p-elementary](#ne6-reduction-to-p-elementary), [NE.3/k1-prime](#ne3-k1-prime), [NE.1/canonical-ore-theorem](#ne1-canonical-ore-theorem).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §5.7, Corollary 90, p. 65 (arXiv v3): Corollary 90, stated after the proofs of Theorems 52–53; no proof is printed.

<a id="ne6-main-conjecture-congruence-criterion"></a>

### NE.6.17. The main conjecture as congruences between abelian zeta functions

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/main-conjecture-congruence-criterion` · theorem.

Let F_∞/F be admissible with μ = 0 and Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G, with Δ cyclic of order prime to p and G a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group; fix Z ≤ G central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z. For P ≤ Ḡ put F_P = F_∞^{Δ×U_P} and K_P = F_∞^{[U_P,U_P]}, so that Gal(K_P/F_P) = Δ × U_P^ab, and let ζ_P ∈ Λ(Δ × U_P^ab)^×_S be the Deligne–Ribet (Cassou-Noguès, Barsky) p-adic zeta function of K_P/F_P (Notations 91). Then MC(F_∞/F) holds if and only if (ζ_P)_P ∈ Φ^G_S, where Φ^G_S = Φ^G_{ℤ_p[Δ],S} (Proposition 93). Every irreducible Artin representation ρ of G has a one-dimensional χ inflated from Γ with ρ ⊗ χ trivial on Z (Lemma 92).

**Hypotheses.**

- F_∞/F admissible, μ = 0, p odd; Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 92 by induction on |G/Z|: by Clifford (Serre, Proposition 24) either ρ|_Z is isotypic, and χ = χ_i^{−1} works, or ρ = Ind_A^G η for a proper A ⊇ Z, and a χ for η on Γ^{p^r} extends to Γ.
2. If: take f ∈ K′₁(Λ(Δ × G)_S) with ∂f = −[C(F_∞/F)] (boundary-surjective-p-torsion). By Theorem 53, θ_S(f) = (f_P) ∈ Φ^G_S. Since ∂ζ_P = ∂f_P = −[C(K_P/F_P)] (abelian-case and base change of C), u_P = ζ_Pf_P^{−1} lies in K′₁(Λ(Δ × U_P^ab)), and (u_P) ∈ Φ^G_S ∩ ∏Λ(Δ × U_P^ab)^× = θ(K′₁(Λ(Δ × G))) (Theorem 53); with θ(u) = (u_P) (Theorem 52), ζ = uf has ∂ζ = −[C(F_∞/F)].
3. Interpolation: for irreducible ρ choose σ by Lemma 92, so ρ ⊗ σ factors through the p-group Ḡ and equals Ind_{U_P}^G η for some P ≤ Ḡ (Serre, Theorem 16: representations of supersolvable groups are monomial); then ρ = Ind_{U_P}^G(η ⊗ σ^{−1}) and ζ(χρκ^r) = ζ_P(χησ^{−1}κ^r_{F_P}) = L_{Σ_P}(χησ^{−1}, 1 − r) = L_Σ(χρ, 1 − r) by inductivity of Artin L-functions.
4. Only if (not printed): if ζ satisfies MC, the components of θ_S(ζ) interpolate the same values as the ζ_P, hence equal them, so (ζ_P) = θ_S(ζ) ∈ Φ^G_S by Theorem 53.

**Check.**

- The printed proof gives the 'if' direction; the converse is the standard argument and is flagged as reconstructed.
- This is burns-kato-patching with Φ = Φ^G, Φ_S = Φ^G_S and the pairs (U_P, [U_P, U_P]).
- The zeta-tuple-in-phi target reduces MC to M1–M4. The zeta-tuple-m1-m2, basic-congruences and m4-from-basic-congruences targets supply the four conditions.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems](#ne6-main-algebraic-theorems), [NE.6/burns-kato-patching](#ne6-burns-kato-patching), [NE.6/abelian-case](#ne6-abelian-case), [NE.5/totally-real-main-conjecture](#ne5-totally-real-main-conjecture), [NE.2/boundary-surjective-p-torsion](#ne2-boundary-surjective-p-torsion), [NE.3/artin-evaluation](#ne3-artin-evaluation), [NE.4/totally-real-iwasawa-complex](#ne4-totally-real-iwasawa-complex), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`, `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.4`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.1, Proposition 93, pp. 66–67 (arXiv v3): Notations 91, Lemma 92 and Proposition 93 with its proof (pp. 66–67), and the statement of Theorem 94 (p. 67).

<a id="ne6-zeta-tuple-m1-m2"></a>

### NE.6.18. The abelian zeta functions satisfy M1 and M2

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/zeta-tuple-m1-m2` · lemma.

In the setting of main-conjecture-congruence-criterion, the tuple (ζ_P)_P satisfies M1: nr^{P′}_P(ζ_{P′}) = π^{P′}_P(ζ_P) in Λ(Δ × U_P/[U_{P′}, U_{P′}])_S whenever [P′, P′] ≤ P ≤ P′; and M2: gζ_Pg^{−1} = ζ_{gPg^{−1}} in Λ(Δ × U_{gPg^{−1}}^ab)_S for every g ∈ G (Proposition 95).

**Hypotheses.**

- As in main-conjecture-congruence-criterion.
- For the Kakde theorem route, p is odd, as in the source’s standing assumption. Toy algebra calculations at 2 do not extend the theorem to 2.

**Construction or proof.**

1. M1: for an irreducible Artin representation ρ of U_P/[U_{P′}, U_{P′}], a character χ of Δ and r divisible by [F_∞(μ_p) : F_∞], nr^{P′}_P(ζ_{P′})(χρκ^r) = ζ_{P′}(χ Ind_{U_P}^{U_{P′}}(ρ)κ^r) = L_{Σ_{P′}}(χ Ind ρ, 1 − r) = L_{Σ_P}(χρ, 1 − r) = π^{P′}_P(ζ_P)(χρκ^r).
2. Two elements of the abelian Λ(Δ × U_P/[U_{P′}, U_{P′}])_S interpolating the same values on this dense set of characters are equal.
3. M2: (gζ_Pg^{−1})(χρκ^r) = ζ_P(χ gρg^{−1} κ^r) = L_Σ(χ Ind_{U_P}^G(gρg^{−1}), 1 − r) and ζ_{gPg^{−1}}(χρκ^r) = L_Σ(χ Ind_{U_{gPg^{−1}}}^G ρ, 1 − r), and the two induced representations agree.

**Check.**

- M3 is the index-p case of the full Möbius congruence. M4 is supplied by the independent completed construction and canonical descent; no claim about validity of Kakde v3 (12) is required.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-conjecture-congruence-criterion](#ne6-main-conjecture-congruence-criterion), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi), [NE.6/abelian-case](#ne6-abelian-case), [NE.3/artin-evaluation](#ne3-artin-evaluation), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`, `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.4`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.1, Proposition 95, pp. 67–68 (arXiv v3): Proposition 95 and its proof (pp. 67–68).

<a id="ne6-partial-zeta-values"></a>

### NE.6.19. Partial zeta values and the smoothed values Δ^u_P

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/partial-zeta-values` · definition.

For j ≥ 0 and a coset x of Z^{p^j} in Δ × U_P^ab, δ(x) is its characteristic function and ζ(δ(x), s) = Σ_𝔞 δ(x)(g_𝔞)N𝔞^{−s} (Re s > 1, over integral ideals 𝔞 of F_P prime to Σ_P, g_𝔞 the Artin symbol) is the partial zeta function; by Klingen and Siegel it continues meromorphically with a simple pole at s = 1 only, and ζ(δ(x), 1 − k) ∈ ℚ for even k ≥ 2. For a locally constant ε = Σ ε(x)δ(x) with values in a ℚ-vector space V, L_{Σ_P}(ε, 1 − k) = Σ_x ε(x)ζ(δ(x), 1 − k) ∈ V (15); for a degree-one Artin character it is the Σ_P-truncated L-value. For ε locally constant ℚ_p-valued, u ∈ U_P^ab and k divisible by [F_∞(μ_p) : F_∞], Δ^u_P(ε, 1 − k) = L_{Σ_P}(ε, 1 − k) − κ(u)^k L_{Σ_P}(ε_u, 1 − k) with ε_u(g) = ε(ug) (16). These values are invariant under conjugation: Δ^u_P(δ(y), 1 − k) = Δ^u_{gPg^{−1}}(δ(gyg^{−1}), 1 − k) for u ∈ Z, g ∈ G (Lemma 103).

**Hypotheses.**

- F_∞/F is admissible with μ = 0 and p odd; Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G with Δ cyclic of order prime to p and G a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group; Z ≤ G is central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z, U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ, F_P = F_∞^{Δ×U_P}, d_P = [F_P : F], r_P = [F_P : ℚ], Σ_P the primes of F_P above Σ, and ζ_P the Deligne–Ribet p-adic zeta function of the abelian extension with group Δ × U_P^ab (Notations 91).

**Construction or proof.**

1. L_{Σ_P}(ε, 1 − k) is well defined: refining j splits δ(x) into the δ of its sub-cosets, and ζ is additive in δ.
2. Lemma 103: for Re s > 1, ζ(δ(gyg^{−1}), s) = Σ_𝔞 δ(y)(g_{𝔞^g})N(𝔞^g)^{−s} = ζ(δ(y), s) by reindexing 𝔞 ↦ 𝔞^g, and meromorphic continuation carries the equality to s = 1 − k; δ(y)_u = δ(u^{−1}y) and u ∈ Z is central.

**API.**

- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.partialZeta` (constructor): ζ(δ(x), s) for a coset x of Z^{p^j}.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.sigmaLValue` (constructor): L_{Σ_P}(ε, 1 − k) for locally constant ε with values in a ℚ-vector space.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.sigmaLValue_linear` (relation): ε ↦ L_{Σ_P}(ε, 1 − k) is ℚ-linear and compatible with refining j.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.smoothedValue` (constructor): Δ^u_P(ε, 1 − k) = L(ε, 1 − k) − κ(u)^k L(ε_u, 1 − k).
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.smoothedValue_conj` (relation): Lemma 103: invariance under conjugation by G.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.sigmaLValue_character` (compatibility): For a degree-one Artin character, the Σ_P-truncated L-value at 1 − k.

**Unit tests.**

- `rational_value`: F_P = ℚ, Σ = {3}, ε = 1 and k = 2: L_Σ(1, −1) = (1 − 3)ζ(−1) = (−2)(−1/12) = 1/6.
- `zero_function`: ε ≡ 0 gives L_{Σ_P}(0, 1 − k) = 0.
- `trivial_u`: u = 1: κ(1) = 1 and ε_1 = ε, so Δ^1_P(ε, 1 − k) = 0; the smoothing needs u of infinite order.
- `odd_k`: For F_P = ℚ and odd k ≥ 3, ζ(1 − k) = 0; the construction uses k divisible by [F_∞(μ_p) : F_∞], which is even.

**Check.**

- The Klingen–Siegel rationality and continuation are cited inputs, requested with the Deligne–Ribet package from AutomorphicPadicLFunctions L3.

**Uses.**

- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/deligne-ribet-approximation: the coefficients of the finite-level approximations of (1 − u)ζ_P
- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/basic-congruence-reduction: the value congruences (18), (22), (24), (28), (29)

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-conjecture-congruence-criterion](#ne6-main-conjecture-congruence-criterion), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`, `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.4`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.3, (15)–(16), pp. 69–70, and Lemma 103, p. 73 (arXiv v3): The definitions (15) and (16) of §6.3 and Lemma 103 of §6.5.

<a id="ne6-deligne-ribet-approximation"></a>

### NE.6.20. Finite-level approximations of the abelian zeta functions

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/deligne-ribet-approximation` · lemma.

Let f be such that κ^{p−1}(Z) = 1 + p^fℤ_p. For P normal in P′ let T_{P,P′,j} be the image of x ↦ Σ_{g∈P′/P} gxg^{−1} on ℤ_p[Δ × U_P^ab/Z^{p^j}]/(p^{f+j}), T_{P,j} = T_{P,N_ḠP,j} (Definition 100). Then Λ(Δ × U_P^ab) ≅ lim_j ℤ_p[Δ × U_P^ab/Z^{p^j}]/(p^{f+j}), and T_{P,P′} ≅ lim_j T_{P,P′,j} (Lemma 101). For u ∈ U_P^ab, j ≥ 0 and k divisible by [F_∞(μ_p) : F_∞], the image of (1 − u)ζ_P in ℤ_p[Δ × U_P^ab/Z^{p^j}]/(p^{f+j}) is Σ_x Δ^u_P(δ(x), 1 − k)κ(x)^{−k}x mod p^{f+j}; in particular the limit does not depend on k (Proposition 102, attributed to Ritter–Weiss).

**Hypotheses.**

- F_∞/F is admissible with μ = 0 and p odd; Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G with Δ cyclic of order prime to p and G a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group; Z ≤ G is central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z, U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ, F_P = F_∞^{Δ×U_P}, d_P = [F_P : F], r_P = [F_P : ℚ], Σ_P the primes of F_P above Σ, and ζ_P the Deligne–Ribet p-adic zeta function of the abelian extension with group Δ × U_P^ab (Notations 91).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 101: a compatible family (x_j) lifts canonically — for t ≥ j the images of x_t in ℤ_p[Δ × U^ab/Z^{p^j}]/(p^{f+t}) converge to x̃_j — and the x̃_j form an element of the limit; for T_{P,P′} pass to the limit in 0 → ker(tr_{P,P′,j}) → ℤ_p[…]/(p^{f+j}) → T_{P,P′,j} → 0, exact on the right because the groups are finite.
2. Step 1: the sums form an inverse system, since κ(x)^k ≡ κ(y)^k mod p^{f+j} when x ↦ y.
3. Step 2: independence of k is congruence (17), Δ^u_P(δ(x), 1 − k)κ(x)^{−k} ≡ Δ^u_P(δ(x), 1 − k′)κ(x)^{−k′} mod p^{f+j}; with η locally constant, η^{[F_∞(μ_p):F_∞]} ≡ κ^{[F_∞(μ_p):F_∞]} mod p^{f+j}, and ε_k = p^{−f−j}η^{−k}δ(x), the function ε_kκ^{k−1} − ε_{k′}κ^{k′−1} is ℤ_p-valued, so Deligne–Ribet Theorem 0.4 applies.
4. Step 3: ζ_u = lim_j(Σ_x Δ^u_P(δ(x), 1 − k)κ(x)^{−k}x) satisfies ζ_u(κ^kε) = Δ^u_P(ε, 1 − k) = (1 − u)ζ_P(κ^kε) for all locally constant ε, by the Deligne–Ribet construction of ζ_P; so (1 − u)ζ_P = ζ_u.

**Check.**

- The Deligne–Ribet congruences (their Theorem 0.4) and the interpolation property of ζ_P are imported from AutomorphicPadicLFunctions L3.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/partial-zeta-values](#ne6-partial-zeta-values), [NE.6/abelian-case](#ne6-abelian-case), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.4, Definition 100, Lemma 101 and Proposition 102, pp. 70–73 (arXiv v3): Definition 100, Lemma 101 and Proposition 102 with its three-step proof.

<a id="ne6-basic-congruence-reduction"></a>

### NE.6.21. Smoothed Möbius values and trace ideals

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/basic-congruence-reduction` · lemma.

In the finite-extension setting of basic-congruence-values, let λ̃_F=2^{−[F:Q]}λ_{F,S}. Fix g first. If its smoothed value congruences hold for that same g for every even locally constant ε, then the image of s_g=Σ_F μ_Σ(Σ_F) ver_F^L((1−g_F)λ̃_F) in Z_p[[H_S^+]] lies in tr_Σ(Z_p[[H_S^+]]). Here H_S^+ is the maximal totally real quotient of H_S. This is an implication from the explicit stabilizer-modulus congruences, rather than the earlier list (18), (22), (24), (28), (29).

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; finite totally real p-extension L/K; same S, transfers, Möbius coefficients, normalization and g_F as in basic-congruence-values.
- The Deligne–Ribet finite-level approximation of each smoothed pseudomeasure holds.
- The quantifier is one fixed g for all ε. The conductor-dependent construction in basic-congruence-values by itself gives no such uniform g.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose cofinal admissible Σ-stable finite quotients H_S/V, with V containing complex conjugations and with cyclotomic precision exceeding all [L:F] exponents plus v_p(|Σ|). The transfer kernels are included before taking a finite quotient; no injectivity of a global transfer is assumed.
2. The coefficient of y is c_y=N_L(y)^{−k} Σ_F μ_Σ(Σ_F) Δ̃_{g_F}(1−[L:F]k, δ_y∘ver_F^L), using N_F(x)^{[L:F]}=N_L(ver_F^L x). If y is not in the image of a transfer the corresponding function is zero.
3. Conjugation carries the term for F to the term for its conjugate, so s_g is Σ-invariant. Apply the value congruence to δ_y, which is even on this quotient, to obtain c_y divisible by |St_Σ(y)|. An invariant element in a finite permutation group ring is a trace precisely when its orbit coefficient is divisible by that stabilizer order.
4. The approximation initially gives this assertion modulo a power of p at least |Σ|. The residual invariant multiple of |Σ| is also a trace. Pass to the inverse limit of the finite trace images; compactness of the integral group rings supplies compatible trace preimages.

**Check.**

- For Σ=C_p and a fixed y, trace membership demands coefficient divisibility by p; for a free orbit it only demands constant coefficients on the orbit.
- The factor 2^{−[F:Q]} is retained until its Möbius-weighted removal in basic-congruences; it is not removed termwise by an unsupported equality.
- The statements ∀ε ∃g and ∃g ∀ε cannot be interchanged. Compactness of trace preimages applies after g has been fixed; it does not supply a common g from conductor-dependent choices.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/deligne-ribet-approximation](#ne6-deligne-ribet-approximation), [NE.6/partial-zeta-values](#ne6-partial-zeta-values).

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-CONGRUENCES-II](#source-ritter-weiss-congruences-ii), §1, Proposition 4 and its full coefficient argument, pp.5–6: The value modulus is exactly the finite permutation-module trace modulus.

<a id="ne6-hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit"></a>

### NE.6.22. Hilbert Eisenstein series, restriction along the diagonal and the q-expansion principle

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit` · lemma.

For L totally real of degree r, Hilbert modular forms of weight k on Γ₀₀(𝔣) have standard q-expansions c(0, f) + Σ_μ c(μ, f)q^μ_L over totally positive μ ∈ 𝒪_L (§6.6). For L′ ⊇ L of relative degree r′=[L′:L], restriction along the diagonal R_{L′/L} maps M_k(Γ₀₀(𝔣𝒪_{L′})) to M_{r′k}(Γ₀₀(𝔣)) with c(μ, R_{L′/L}f) = Σ_{tr(ν)=μ} c(ν, f) (§6.7), and its constant term at the cusp of α is that of f at α^* (Lemma 109). For β ∈ 𝒪_L totally positive with 𝔣 ⊆ β𝒪_L, the Hecke operator U_β has c(μ, f|U_β) = c(μβ, f) (Lemma 110). Deligne–Ribet: for locally constant ε on Gal(L_Σ/L) and even k ≥ 2 there is G_{k,ε} ∈ M_k(Γ₀₀(𝔣)) with q-expansion 2^{−r}L(ε, 1 − k) + Σ_μ(Σ_{𝔞∋μ} ε(g_𝔞)N𝔞^{k−1})q^μ and constant term N^k((α))2^{−r}L(ε_g, 1 − k) at the cusp of α (Proposition 111); the q-expansion principle: if f ∈ M_k(Γ₀₀(𝔣), ℚ) has non-constant standard coefficients in ℤ_(p), then c(0, f) − N(α_p)^{−k}c(0, α, f) ∈ ℤ_p (§6.11); hence c(0, G_{k,ε}) − N(α_p)^{−k}c(0, α, G_{k,ε}) = 2^{−r}Δ^u(ε, 1 − k) when α has Artin symbol u (Remark 112).

**Hypotheses.**

- L totally real; Σ a finite set of finite primes containing those above p; 𝔣 an integral ideal supported on Σ.
- For the Kakde theorem route, p is odd, as in the source’s standing assumption. Toy algebra calculations at 2 do not extend the theorem to 2.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Lemma 109: (R_{L′/L}f)|α = R_{L′/L}(f|α^*), and constant terms are read off at τ → i∞.
2. Lemma 110: U_β is the double coset of diag(β, 1), f|U_β(τ) = N(β)^{k/2−1}Σ_b f|(1 b; 0 β), and the character sum Σ_b e^{2πi tr(μb/β)} is N(β) for μ ∈ β𝒪_L and 0 otherwise.
3. Proposition 111 and the q-expansion principle are Deligne–Ribet (Proposition 6.1, 2.22, 0.3 and 5.13–5.15); Remark 112 combines them with N((α))^kN(α_p)^{−k} = κ(u)^k.

**Check.**

- Deligne–Ribet's Eisenstein series, their constant terms at cusps and the q-expansion principle are requested from AutomorphicPadicLFunctions L3, which the atlas makes their owner.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/partial-zeta-values](#ne6-partial-zeta-values), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §§6.6–6.11, Lemmas 109–110, Proposition 111 and Remark 112, pp. 77–81 (arXiv v3): The Hilbert-modular toolkit of §§6.6–6.11; Lemmas 109 and 110 are proved in the text, Proposition 111 and the q-expansion principle are cited to Deligne–Ribet.

<a id="ne6-transfer-image-not-generator"></a>

### NE.6.23. The transfer into a maximal cyclic subgroup misses its generators

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/transfer-image-not-generator` · lemma.

Let P ∈ C(Ḡ) be such that no P′ ∈ C(Ḡ) has P′^p = P, and let P ⊊ N ≤ N_ḠP. Then the image of the transfer ver : N^ab → P is a proper subgroup of P (Lemma 114).

**Hypotheses.**

- Ḡ a finite p-group.
- For the Kakde theorem route, p is odd, as in the source’s standing assumption. Toy algebra calculations at 2 do not extend the theorem to 2.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For g∈N, put m=ord(gP) in N/P, the length of each orbit on N/P (P is normal in N). With one representative x_j per ⟨g⟩-orbit, transfer is ∏_j x_j⁻¹g^m x_j. The order of g itself is not the orbit length.
2. If g∉P and g^m generated P, then ⟨g⟩ would be cyclic with a cyclic subgroup above P whose p-th powers equal P, contradicting maximality. Thus every factor lies in P^p and their product does too.
3. If g ∈ P, ver(g) = ∏_{x∈N/P} x^{−1}gx; N acts trivially on the order-p subgroup of P, so ver(g)^{p^{r−1}} = ∏ g^{p^{r−1}} = g^{p^{r−1}[N:P]} = 1 for #⟨g⟩ = p^r, since p divides [N : P].

**Check.**

- Used in Proposition 115: nontrivial stabilisers kill δ(y)(g_𝔟).

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:MonoidHom.transfer`.

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.12, Lemma 114, pp. 82–83 (arXiv v3): Lemma 114 and its proof.

<a id="ne6-basic-congruence-values"></a>

### NE.6.24. Möbius-weighted Hilbert Eisenstein value congruences

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/basic-congruence-values` · theorem.

Let L/K be a finite p-extension of totally real number fields, Σ=Gal(L/K), and S contain p and every ramified finite prime. Put Σ_F=Gal(L/F), g_F=ver_K^F(g), and use the smoothed values of 2^{−[F:Q]}ζ_{F,S}, denoted by Δ̃. For every positive even k and even locally constant Z_(p)-valued ε on H_S=Gal(L_S/L), the special g constructed below satisfies Σ_{K⊆F⊆L} μ_Σ(Σ_F) Δ̃_{g_F}(1−|Σ_F|k, ε∘ver_F^L) ∈ |St_Σ(ε)| Z_(p). Here μ_Σ(V)=μ(1,V) is the subgroup-poset Möbius coefficient and even means factoring through the maximal totally real quotient. This replaces the unsupported cyclic-overgroup sum of Kakde v3 (12); it is not a claim to repair that false sum by changing weights.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; L/K and S as in the statement. No μ=0 hypothesis is needed for this finite analytic congruence.
- Choose a common conductor ideal f supported on S, divisible by |Σ|, for all ε∘ver_F^L. Choose a positive rational integer f₀∈f, and the rational finite idèle α with components 1+f₀ at primes dividing f₀ and 1 elsewhere. Let γ=α_K and g=Art_K(γ). Then γ and γ⁻¹ lie in 1+f̂ and N_F(g_F)=(1+f₀)^{−[F:Q]}, so every g_F has nontrivial cyclotomic image.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For each intermediate field form G_{[L:F]k, ε∘ver_F^L}, restrict from F to K, then apply U_[F:K]. The common weight is [F:K][L:F]k=[L:K]k, and the q^a coefficient sums over (β,b) with β∈b⊆O_F, b prime to S and Tr_{F/K}(β)=[F:K]a. Keep these relative degrees; an absolute degree from a different bottom field cannot replace them.
2. Identify these pairs with the Σ_F-fixed pairs in L. Because S includes every ramified prime, an invariant ideal prime to S descends to the fixed field. For a pair with stabilizer P≤Σ the full subgroup sum contributes ε(b)N_L(b)^k Σ_{V≤P} μ_P(V) r^[P:V], where r=N_L(b)^(−1/|P|) is a rational p-unit, obtained by descending b to L^P. This uses every subgroup V≤P with its multiplicity.
3. For P≠1 take the Teichmüller lift z of r modulo p. Then r^{p^n}≡z modulo p^{n+1}. The finite p-group Möbius identity gives |V| divides p μ_P(V): μ(1,V)=0 unless V is elementary abelian, and for rank d its value is (−1)^d p^{d(d−1)/2}. This identity is a finite subgroup-poset induction using the Frattini quotient and the Gaussian-binomial recursion; include that combinatorial lemma in the supplier proof, not an analytic assumption. Each summand is congruent to μ_P(V)z modulo |P|. Their sum vanishes since Σ_{V≤P} μ_P(V)=0. For P=1 the required modulus is 1 and the assertion is automatic.
4. Group pairs under St_Σ(ε). An orbit has |St_Σ(ε)|/|St_Σ(ε)∩P| elements, while its single-pair contribution is divisible by |P|, hence by |St_Σ(ε)∩P|. Every nonconstant coefficient of the Möbius sum E is divisible by |St_Σ(ε)|.
5. Use only the special cusp γ above. The matrix diag(γ,γ⁻¹) belongs to the completed level group, so its second factor M₂ in the Deligne–Ribet cusp decomposition is the identity. Thus applying U_[F:K] preserves the constant coefficient at this cusp, by its standard-cusp coefficient formula. This proves the required Hecke comparison at these cusps; no arbitrary-cusp assertion is used.
6. The normalized cusp expansion multiplies the constant coefficient by N_K(g)^{[L:K]k}=N_F(g_F)^{[L:F]k}. Therefore the constant coefficient of E(1)−E(γ) is exactly the stated sum of Δ̃ values, including each smoothing exponent and each relative weight. The q-expansion principle transfers the nonconstant-coefficient divisibility to this constant difference.

**Check.**

- For Σ=C_p×C_p the coefficients are 1 at 1, −1 at each of the p+1 lines, and p at Σ. At p=3 and r=2 the stabilizer polynomial is 2^9−4·2^3+3·2=486, divisible by 9. Replacing the full Möbius sum by an unweighted sum of cyclic overgroups fails this test.
- For a proper normalizer N in a larger finite quotient, restriction to its fixed field multiplies a weight by [N:P], not [Ḡ:P]. The special-cusp norm identity must use the same bottom field as that restriction.
- For the full stabilizer P=1 no cancellation Σμ=0 is asserted: divisibility modulo 1 is enough. For P≠1 retain all fixed pairs, including β rational and b=O_L.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit](#ne6-hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit), [NE.6/partial-zeta-values](#ne6-partial-zeta-values).

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-CONGRUENCES-II](#source-ritter-weiss-congruences-ii), Propositions 5 and 9, pp.6–9; Lemma 10 and conclusion, pp.10–11 (arXiv v3): Relative restriction weights, complete stabilizer sums and the special-cusp Hecke calculation prove the stated value congruence.
- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), Propositions 115–117, pp.83–86; Proposition 98, (12), p.69 (arXiv v3): Comparison only: this revision does not use the cyclic-overgroup sum, its fixed-pair cancellation, or the omitted proof of (29). See E33–E34.

<a id="ne6-basic-congruences"></a>

### NE.6.25. Analytic Möbius and index-p transfer congruences

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/basic-congruences` · theorem.

Let G be a one-dimensional p-elementary totally real Galois group, A an abelian normal open subgroup containing its prime-to-p cyclic factor, and Q=G/A. In the central localization Λ•A, Σ_{A≤U≤G} μ_Q(U/A) ver_A^{U^ab}(ζ_{U^ab}) belongs to tr_Q(Λ•A), with the same S-truncation on every abelian pseudomeasure. For |Q|=p this is the index-p transfer congruence M3. In the finite-over-cyclotomic pro-p setting, central localization by central elements regular modulo p agrees with the canonical S-localization; unramified coefficient factors are treated componentwise.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; totally real extension finite over the cyclotomic tower; S contains p and all ramified primes.
- Use abelian pseudomeasures with the Deligne–Ribet normalization; μ=0 is required when viewing them as units in the completed/canonical main-conjecture construction, not for the finite analytic value congruence.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use Ritter–Weiss Congruences II, Theorem p.2, for a single g with nontrivial cyclotomic transfer in every intermediate field and with the smoothed Möbius sum in the trace ideal. The coefficient reduction is basic-congruence-reduction; basic-congruence-values explains the conductor-dependent Hilbert calculation. Its per-function choice of cusp does not by itself prove the theorem’s uniform existence assertion. That precise source-theorem input remains an independent proof-audit obligation below. Work on the universal abelian S-ramified groups and their totally real quotient, with L the fixed field of A.
2. Remove the tilde normalization in the Möbius sum. For V=Gal(L/F), the difference μ_Q(V)(2^{−[F:Q]}−2^{−[L:Q]}) is divisible by |Q|. Indeed |V| divides p μ_Q(V), and for V≠1 the factor (2^{|V|−1})^{[F:Q]}−1 has p-adic valuation at least 1+v_p([F:Q]); for V=1 the difference is zero. Its invariant sum is a trace. This only uses the p-primary modulus, not divisibility modulo [F:Q].
3. Put h=ver_K^L(g). Each summand has the common factor 1−h. Localize the universal group ring only at Z_p[[closure〈h〉]]∖p, then map to Λ•A. The cyclotomic image of h is nontrivial, so 1−h becomes a unit there. This is the deflation of a pseudomeasure in Ritter–Weiss Lemma c; it also works when A has a prime-to-p factor. The entire universal augmentation localization need not map to that factor and is not used.
4. The transfer/deflation square follows from corresponding coset representatives in G(L_S/K) and its quotient G. Divide the smoothed sum by the Q-fixed unit 1−h after this square; trace ideals commute with this central localization. This proves the displayed ordinary-pseudomeasure congruence.
5. To obtain Kakde M3 for P≤P′ of index p, apply the index-p case to U_{P′}/[U_P,U_P], whose normal subgroup U_P^ab is abelian. The full G need not itself be abelian.

**Check.**

- For Q=C_p this gives ζ_A−ver_A^{G^ab}(ζ_{G^ab}) in tr_Q, with the sign determined by μ_Q(Q)=−1.
- Kakde v3 (12) is not a target: take Ḡ=C_{p²}×C_p, P=pC_{p²}×0. There are p cyclic P′ with P′^p=P, and W_Ḡ(P) has order p² acting trivially. M3 implies ζ_P≡φ(ζ_{P′}) modulo p for each P′, whereas (12) would imply ζ_P≡pζ_P=0 modulo p, contradicting that ζ_P is a unit. The corrected full Möbius sum preserves the missing multiplicity.
- Retain the uniform existence assertion of the named theorem, not a deduction of ∃g ∀ε from ∀ε ∃g. No claim is made that the conductor-dependent calculation alone discharges this assertion.
- Quantifier test. The g that work form a closed subgroup: s_(gh)=s_g+ver_K^L(g)s_h, because ver_F^L is a ring map and ver_K^L(g) is Σ-invariant. Proposition 5's element for the conductor 𝔣 has trivial image at that level, and its norm tends to 1 as 𝔣 grows. So no fixed g follows from the per-conductor construction (obligation 19).

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/basic-congruence-reduction](#ne6-basic-congruence-reduction), [NE.6/basic-congruence-values](#ne6-basic-congruence-values), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi).

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Proposition 6, p.3; §3, Lemma c and (3.1)–(3.3), pp.10–12: Normalization removal, legitimate pseudomeasure deflation and division by the common smoothing factor.
- [RITTER-WEISS-CONGRUENCES-II](#source-ritter-weiss-congruences-ii), Theorem, p.2, and concluding Remark, p.11: The special-g congruence and the limitation on universal localization with a prime-to-p factor.

<a id="ne6-enlarged-extension"></a>

### NE.6.26. The auxiliary extension F̃_∞ and the trace-ideal bound

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/enlarged-extension` · lemma.

Choose a prime l ≡ 1 mod |Ḡ| with ℚ(μ_l) ∩ F_∞ = ℚ, let K ⊆ ℚ(μ_l) have degree |Ḡ| over ℚ, and put F̃ = KF, F̃_∞ = F̃F_∞. Then Gal(F̃_∞/F) = H̃ × Δ × G with H̃ cyclic of order |Ḡ|, and G̃/Z ≅ H̃ × Δ × Ḡ. For P ∈ C(G̃) let i_P = max{[P′ : P] : P ⊆ P′ ∈ C(G̃)}. If P ≠ {1} then T_P ⊆ p·i_P²·Λ(Δ × U_P), and T_{1} = |G̃|Λ(Δ × Z); the same holds for T_{P,S} and T̂_P (Lemma 118). The main conjecture for F̃_∞/F implies it for F_∞/F.

**Hypotheses.**

- F_∞/F is admissible with μ = 0 and p odd; Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G with Δ cyclic of order prime to p and G a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group; Z ≤ G is central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z, U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ, F_P = F_∞^{Δ×U_P}, d_P = [F_P : F], r_P = [F_P : ℚ], Σ_P the primes of F_P above Σ, and ζ_P the Deligne–Ribet p-adic zeta function of the abelian extension with group Δ × U_P^ab (Notations 91).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Case P ⊆ H̃: i_P = [H̃ : P] and N_{G̃}P = G̃ acts trivially on Λ(Δ × U_P), so T_P = [G̃ : P]Λ = |Ḡ|[H̃ : P]Λ ⊆ p·i_P²Λ since |Ḡ| ≥ p·i_P.
2. Case P ⊄ H̃: P = ⟨(h̃, h)⟩ with h ≠ 1; for P′ = ⟨(h̃₀, h₀)⟩ realising i_P, H̃ × ⟨h₀⟩ ⊆ N_{G̃}P acts trivially, so T_P ⊆ (|H̃||⟨h₀⟩|/|P|)Λ = |H̃|i_PΛ ⊆ p·i_P²Λ as |H̃| ≥ p·i_P.
3. Admissibility and μ = 0 for F̃_∞, which the source asserts without argument (E10): K is totally real because |Ḡ| is odd and divides (l − 1)/2, and μ = 0 passes from F_∞ to the finite p-extension F̃_∞ (the vanishing of μ is inherited by finite p-extensions, a theorem of Iwasawa, cited here).
4. Descent: MC for F̃_∞/F gives MC for its quotient F_∞/F by the compatibility of ∂, C and interpolation with the quotient map (as in reduction-to-dimension-one).

**Check.**

- The descent along G̃ → G uses Fukaya–Kato's base change for C, recorded in the gap on Fukaya–Kato.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-conjecture-congruence-criterion](#ne6-main-conjecture-congruence-criterion), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi), [NE.6/reduction-to-dimension-one](#ne6-reduction-to-dimension-one), [NE.4/admissible-extension](#ne4-admissible-extension).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §§6.13.1–6.13.2, Lemma 118, pp. 86–87 (arXiv v3): The trick of §6.13, the field F̃_∞ and Lemma 118 with its proof.

<a id="ne6-completed-zeta-canonical-descent"></a>

### NE.6.27. Descent of a completed zeta element to the canonical localization

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/completed-zeta-canonical-descent` · comparison.

Let A_G=Λ_O(G), with G one-dimensional pro-p, finite kernel over Γ, central open Z≅Z_p and O an unramified coefficient domain. Let B_G be the p-adic completion of the central-regular localization of A_G. If b∈K₁(B_G) has abelianized norms equal to the normalized abelian zeta functions ζ_P, then there is ζ∈K′₁((A_G)_S) with θ_S(ζ)=(ζ_P) and ∂ζ=−[C]. In particular that tuple satisfies M1–M4. The result uses abelian main conjectures and arithmetic base change to impose its boundary; it does not identify the Ritter–Weiss refined class with [C].

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; finite-over-cyclotomic totally real μ=0 setting; A_G and C as in the canonical main-conjecture criterion.
- The integral θ-isomorphism K′₁(A_G)≅Φ, the inclusion θ̂(K₁(B_G))⊆Φ̂, the surjective canonical localization boundary, and the abelian main conjectures with base change of C are available.
- All denominator sets and coefficient products are componentwise. For Δ×G, apply the argument to the unramified domains O in Z_p[Δ].

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose f∈K′₁((A_G)_S) with ∂f=−[C], and representatives in K₁ when mapping to B_G. The base-change boundary square and the abelian main conjecture give u_P=ζ_P/θ_S(f)_P∈Λ_O(U_P^ab)^×. Abelian semilocal K₁ is its unit group, so this asserts integrality of the element and its inverse.
2. The ratio b/f_B has completed θ-image u, so u∈Φ̂. Prove the contraction Φ̂∩∏_P Λ_O(U_P^ab)^×=Φ. Put R=O[[Z]], and R̂ the p-completed localization of R at R∖pR. The map R/p^n→R̂/p^n is injective: a denominator nonzero modulo p is a non-zero-divisor successively on p^i/p^{i+1}. All rings in the θ-diagram are finite free over R.
3. For a trace condition let W act on U_P^ab, with Z embedded centrally. Its finite coset set U_P^ab/Z admits W-equivariant lifts: a coset stabilizer acts on a lift by a homomorphism to the torsion-free group Z, and its finite image must be zero. Choose lifts orbit by orbit. In this permutation R-basis the trace image on an orbit is generated by |St_W(v)| times the orbit sum. Thus trace membership is exactly coefficient divisibility by a power of p, which contracts under R→R̂; the same holds for p times the trace ideal. The embedding of Z is valid because [U_P,U_P] lies in the finite cyclotomic kernel.
4. M1 and M2 contract by injectivity. M3 contracts by the preceding trace computation. M4 contracts by that computation for pT, since the α and Frobenius products of integral units are integral. This proves the intersection, without inventing a map B_G→(A_G)_S or calling completed group units torsion.
5. Lift u to z∈K′₁(A_G) by the integral θ-isomorphism. Then ζ=zf has θ-image ζ_P and boundary −[C]. Its image under θ_S lies in Φ_S by the algebraic image theorem. Uniqueness is the separate main-conjecture criterion.

**Check.**

- If W acts trivially with |W|=p^a, the trace ideal is exactly p^a times the ring; completion cannot weaken this modulus.
- For a free W-orbit the trace generator is its orbit sum with coefficient 1. No false divisibility by |W| is imposed.
- The lift f is generally not integral and b need not come from a canonical localized element by an inverse completion map. The correction z is integral because its entire tuple u, including inverses, is integral.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems](#ne6-main-algebraic-theorems), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi), [NE.6/abelian-case](#ne6-abelian-case), [NE.2/boundary-surjective-p-torsion](#ne2-boundary-surjective-p-torsion), [NE.4/totally-real-iwasawa-complex](#ne4-totally-real-iwasawa-complex), [NE.4/equivariant-perfect-base-change](#ne4-equivariant-perfect-base-change).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), Lemma 85, pp.58–60; Theorems 52–53, p.36, proved pp.64–65; Proposition 93, pp.66–67 (arXiv v3): Completed image inclusion and integral θ lifting; the contraction/boundary patching above supplies the comparison.
- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), §4, Lemma e, pp.13–14: The same equivariant permutation-basis argument over a central procyclic subgroup.
- [VENJAKOB-COMPARISON-2011](#source-venjakob-comparison-2011), §§1–2, pp.1–5; §6, Theorems 6.2–6.6, pp.18–22: Comparison of A(G), B(G) and the additive/multiplicative images; no unstated inverse completion map.

<a id="ne6-m4-from-basic-congruences"></a>

### NE.6.28. M4 from Möbius congruences and canonical descent

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/m4-from-basic-congruences` · lemma.

In the one-dimensional Δ×pro-p, odd-p, totally real μ=0 setting, the normalized abelian zeta tuple satisfies M4 in the canonical localization. This is proved through the full Möbius congruences, the independent Ritter–Weiss completed construction and completed-zeta-canonical-descent. The branch through Kakde v3 Proposition 98 and Lemma 119 is replaced; it is not an assumption of this result.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; totally real extension finite over the cyclotomic tower; S contains p and all ramified primes.
- μ=0; abelian main conjectures, arithmetic base change and the corrected algebraic θ-image theorem; unramified coefficient factors treated separately.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The analytic and algebraic full Möbius congruences feed the Ritter–Weiss logarithmic integrality/support-reduction proof. Its torsion correction yields b∈K₁(B_G) with Det b=L, without assuming any Kakde M4 theorem for zeta functions.
2. Normalized abelian restriction identifies θ̂_P(b)=ζ_P for every P. Apply completed-zeta-canonical-descent to obtain ζ∈K′₁(Λ_O(G)_S) with that tuple and the required boundary.
3. The algebraic inclusion θ_S(K′₁(Λ_O(G)_S))⊆Φ_S, already proved independently of arithmetic congruences, gives M4. At nontrivial cyclic P it reads α_P(ζ_P)/∏_{P′^p=P, P′≠P} φ(α_{P′}(ζ_{P′}))∈1+pT_{P,S}. At P=1 the numerator is ζ_1^p/φ(ζ_1), rather than the nontrivial-character definition of α_1.
4. There is no dependency on zeta-tuple-in-phi or kakde-main-theorem in the completed construction or descent comparison, so this route does not use the desired arithmetic M4 to prove itself. No auxiliary-extension trace denominator is needed for this replacement.

**Check.**

- The C_{p²}×C_p test in basic-congruences excludes the old cyclic-overgroup sum. M4 here follows from an actual completed multiplicative preimage and contraction of integral trace conditions.
- The special central P=1 factor is retained, while scalar torsion is embedded with norm powers [G:U_P].

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/rw-main-theorem](#ne6-rw-main-theorem), [NE.6/completed-zeta-canonical-descent](#ne6-completed-zeta-canonical-descent), [NE.6/congruence-group-phi](#ne6-congruence-group-phi).

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Propositions 3, 5–6, pp.2–3; Theorem 7, p.4; §§4–6, pp.13–25: Independent completed multiplicative construction from full Möbius congruences, including torsion information.
- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), Lemma 85, pp.58–60; Theorems 52–53, p.36 (arXiv v3): Algebraic image inclusion supplies M4 after descent, independently of the rejected analytic (12).

<a id="ne6-zeta-tuple-in-phi"></a>

### NE.6.29. The abelian zeta functions satisfy M1–M4

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/zeta-tuple-in-phi` · theorem.

For the original one-dimensional Δ×pro-p extension, the tuple (ζ_P) lies in Φ_S: M1–M2 follow from interpolation and conjugation, M3 from the index-p Möbius congruence, and M4 from the completed construction and canonical descent. The same statement applies to the separate enlarged extension. The canonical-localization main conjecture follows from the congruence criterion.

**Hypotheses.**

- F_∞/F is admissible with μ = 0 and p odd; Gal(F_∞/F) = Δ × G with Δ cyclic of order prime to p and G a one-dimensional pro-p p-adic Lie group; Z ≤ G is central open pro-cyclic, Ḡ = G/Z, U_P ≤ G the preimage of P ≤ Ḡ, F_P = F_∞^{Δ×U_P}, d_P = [F_P : F], r_P = [F_P : ℚ], Σ_P the primes of F_P above Σ, and ζ_P the Deligne–Ribet p-adic zeta function of the abelian extension with group Δ × U_P^ab (Notations 91).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use zeta-tuple-m1-m2 and basic-congruences for M1–M3.
2. Use m4-from-basic-congruences, with the replacement proof through completed-zeta-canonical-descent, for M4.
3. Apply the independent algebraic main-conjecture-congruence-criterion. The separate enlarged-extension descent remains valid but is unnecessary for this Möbius-based argument.

**Check.**

- This proves the target asserted in Kakde Theorem 94 by a replacement analytic branch; it does not validate Proposition 98 or its claimed fixed-pair cancellation.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/zeta-tuple-m1-m2](#ne6-zeta-tuple-m1-m2), [NE.6/basic-congruences](#ne6-basic-congruences), [NE.6/m4-from-basic-congruences](#ne6-m4-from-basic-congruences), [NE.6/main-conjecture-congruence-criterion](#ne6-main-conjecture-congruence-criterion).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §6.1, Theorem 94, p. 67, with §6.13, pp. 86–90 (arXiv v3): Theorem 94 (M1–M4, whose M4 display is a quotient, as the page image shows) and its proof assembled in §§6.2–6.13.
- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Theorem, p.1; Proposition 3, p.2; §§4–5, pp.13–22: Independent completed construction used in the replacement proof.

<a id="ne6-kakde-main-theorem"></a>

### NE.6.30. The main conjecture for totally real fields (Kakde)

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/kakde-main-theorem` · theorem.

Let p be odd and F_∞/F an admissible p-adic Lie extension satisfying μ = 0, with G = Gal(F_∞/F). There is a unique ζ(F_∞/F) ∈ K′₁(Λ(G)_S) with ∂(ζ(F_∞/F)) = −[C(F_∞/F)] and ζ(F_∞/F)(ρκ^r_F) = L_Σ(ρ, 1 − r) for every Artin character ρ of G and every r ≥ 1 divisible by [F_∞(μ_p) : F_∞] (Theorem 11). That is, MC(F_∞/F) of NE.5/totally-real-main-conjecture holds.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; F_∞/F admissible; μ = 0 (Definition 8, known when F_∞ is abelian over ℚ by Ferrero–Washington).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduce to dimension one (reduction-to-dimension-one), then to ℚ_p-elementary groups (reduction-to-qp-elementary).
2. The l-ℚ_p-elementary case for l ≠ p is l-elementary-case; the p-ℚ_p-elementary case reduces to Δ × G_p with G_p one-dimensional pro-p (reduction-to-p-elementary).
3. For Δ×G_p use the corrected algebraic θ theorem and the replacement full-Möbius/completed-descent proof of zeta-tuple-in-phi, directly for the original extension. The K′₁-injectivity hypotheses of the elementary reductions are k1-injects-localisation; handle Z_p[Δ] in its unramified domain factors.
4. Uniqueness: two solutions differ by an element of K′₁(Λ(G)) (∂ = 0 and Corollary 90) with trivial evaluations, which is 1 by the diagram chases of the reductions.

**Check.**

- The target of Kakde Theorem 11 is preserved. The analytic branch through v3 (12) is replaced by the Ritter–Weiss full Möbius construction and the explicit canonical-descent comparison. This is a combined proof plan, not a claim to have verified every original intermediate congruence.
- Finite-group Oliver/Wall, arithmetic perfectness and the earlier logarithmic supplier leaves remain explicitly named. The independent-review ledger is historical and has not been self-approved by this revision.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/zeta-tuple-in-phi](#ne6-zeta-tuple-in-phi), [NE.6/enlarged-extension](#ne6-enlarged-extension), [NE.6/reduction-to-dimension-one](#ne6-reduction-to-dimension-one), [NE.6/reduction-to-qp-elementary](#ne6-reduction-to-qp-elementary), [NE.6/l-elementary-case](#ne6-l-elementary-case), [NE.6/reduction-to-p-elementary](#ne6-reduction-to-p-elementary), [NE.6/k1-injects-localisation](#ne6-k1-injects-localisation), [NE.6/main-algebraic-theorems](#ne6-main-algebraic-theorems), [NE.6/abelian-case](#ne6-abelian-case), [NE.5/totally-real-main-conjecture](#ne5-totally-real-main-conjecture).

**Sources.**

- [KAKDE-2011](#source-kakde-2011), §2.5, Theorem 11, p. 13 (arXiv v3); proof completed on p. 90: Theorem 11 gives the target. This revision preserves that statement and replaces the cyclic-overgroup analytic branch in its proof.
- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Propositions 3 and 6, pp.2–3; §§4–5, pp.13–22: Replacement analytic/completed proof in the dimension-one p-elementary case.

**Atlas planet:** Kakde's main conjecture for totally real fields.

<a id="ne6-rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure"></a>

### NE.6.31. Ritter–Weiss logarithmic pseudomeasure

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure` · construction.

In the one-dimensional Ritter–Weiss setting, let Λ^G be the p-adic completion after inverting central elements regular modulo p, Q^G its total quotient, and T(R)=R/[R,R]_add the additive commutator quotient. The reduced-trace character map Tr identifies T(Q^G) with the admissible character-function space. Define t_(K/k) by Tr(t)=L(L_(K/k)), where L(f)(χ)=p⁻¹ log(f(χ)^p/Ψ(f(ψ_pχ))) and Ψ is induced by γ↦γ^p. For p-elementary G the integral group logarithm satisfies Tr∘L=L∘Det and has image in T(Λ^G). This completion and this t are distinct from the canonical localization and a multiplicative zeta element.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; K/k totally real, k/Q finite and K/k_cyc finite; the stated character functions satisfy Galois and cyclotomic twisting compatibilities.
- For integrality of the group logarithm use p-elementary G=C×P with C finite cyclic of order prime to p and P pro-p.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Import reduced trace and the additive commutator quotient; form the central localization and p-adic completion.
2. Use the reduced-trace character isomorphism to define t uniquely.
3. Use the integral logarithm square and Adams/Frobenius normalization from Ritter–Weiss diagram (D1); its earlier-paper inputs are recorded as a gap.

**API.**

- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.RWLogarithmicPseudomeasure.ofCharacterFunction` (constructor): t is the inverse reduced-trace image of L(L_(K/k)).
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.RWLogarithmicPseudomeasure.trace` (characterisation): Tr(t)=L(L_(K/k)); injectivity of Tr gives uniqueness in the additive quotient.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.RWLogarithmicPseudomeasure.deflation` (functoriality): Deflation and the source-defined additive restriction commute with t and with the trace character maps.
- `TauCeti.NoncommIwasawa.RWLogarithmicPseudomeasure.logDet` (compatibility): Tr(L(x))=L(Det(x)); additive restriction is the Ritter–Weiss map, not naive deletion of basis terms.

**Unit tests.**

- `rw_logarithmic_pseudomeasure_1`: For an abelian group, T(Λ^G)=Λ^G and the trace comparison is the scalar character comparison.
- `rw_logarithmic_pseudomeasure_2`: For the group-like unit γ, p⁻¹log(γ^p/Ψγ)=0; a logarithm omitting Ψ fails this test.
- `rw_logarithmic_pseudomeasure_3`: For the scalar unit 1+p over Z_p, the logarithm is (1−p⁻¹)log(1+p), not the ordinary log.
- `rw_logarithmic_pseudomeasure_4`: The zero additive pseudomeasure is uniquely determined by zero trace but does not imply uniqueness of a multiplicative preimage when SK₁ survives.

**Check.**

- For an abelian group, T(Λ^G)=Λ^G and the trace comparison is the scalar character comparison.
- For the group-like unit γ, p⁻¹log(γ^p/Ψγ)=0; a logarithm omitting Ψ fails this test.
- For the scalar unit 1+p over Z_p, the logarithm is (1−p⁻¹)log(1+p), not the ordinary log.
- The zero additive pseudomeasure is uniquely determined by zero trace but does not imply uniqueness of a multiplicative preimage when SK₁ survives.

**Uses.**

- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-logarithmic-integrality: turns existence into an additive integrality test
- NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-main-theorem: supplies the support-reduction argument

**Prerequisites:** [NE.3/semisimple-total-quotient](#ne3-semisimple-total-quotient), [NE.3/reduced-norm-sk1-comparison](#ne3-reduced-norm-sk1-comparison), [NE.6/integral-logarithm](#ne6-integral-logarithm), `KTheoryLowDegrees:U.5`.

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), §1, diagram (D1), pp. 4–5; §6, Lemma i, pp. 22–24: Defines the additive logarithmic object and gives the integral logarithm comparison, with references to the earlier papers for the general trace character space.

<a id="ne6-rw-mobius-congruences"></a>

### NE.6.32. Möbius–Wall and abelian pseudomeasure congruences

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-mobius-congruences` · theorem.

Let A be an abelian normal open subgroup of p-elementary G and Q=G/A a finite p-group. For ε∈(Λ^G)^×, Σ_(A⊆U⊆G) μ_Q(U/A) ver_A^U(res_U^G ε) lies in tr_Q(Λ^A). The same Möbius sum with the abelian pseudomeasures λ_(K^[U,U]/K^U) lies in the corresponding trace ideal in the central localization Λ•A. Here res denotes the K₁ transfer before the abelian group-transfer map ver, and μ_Q is the subgroup-poset Möbius function. These are distinct algebraic and analytic congruences.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; one-dimensional totally real Ritter–Weiss setting; A includes the prime-to-p cyclic direct factor.
- The analytic clause uses the S-truncated abelian pseudomeasures and their Deligne–Ribet congruences.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For the algebraic clause, expand the restriction determinant using representatives of G/A and the associated group-extension cocycle. For each coset permutation group the coefficients into orbits under Q. Its cocycle factor is invariant under the permutation stabilizer (Ritter–Weiss §2, Lemma a and the claim after (2.5)).
2. The remaining stabilizer sum is the Möbius sum in §2 Lemma b. Prove that lemma by induction on subgroup order: split a coefficient as e₀+e₁, expand into functions on the coset set with values in {0,1}, and group nonconstant functions by their proper stabilizers. Their contributions are iterated traces by induction; constant functions cancel against the two pure terms. This retains all subgroups and conjugacy multiplicities.
3. For the analytic clause use basic-congruences, whose Hilbert coefficient and special-cusp calculation is supplied above, with the uniform smoothed theorem retained as a precisely stated primary-source input. The algebraic and analytic sums have the same subgroup indexing and target trace ideal; neither is the unweighted cyclic-overgroup sum of Kakde (12).

**Check.**

- If Q=C_p, μ_Q(1)=1 and μ_Q(Q)=−1, giving the norm-transfer difference in the Q-trace ideal.
- If Q=C_p², the subgroup Möbius coefficients are 1 on 1, −1 on each line and p on Q.
- If A=G, the trace ideal is all Λ^A; the resulting congruence imposes no extra condition.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure](#ne6-rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure), [NE.6/hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit](#ne6-hilbert-eisenstein-toolkit), `AutomorphicPadicLFunctions:L3`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/InductionRestriction#layer-6-the-virtual-character-ring-artin-and-brauer-induction`, [NE.6/basic-congruences](#ne6-basic-congruences).

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Propositions 5–6, p. 3; §2, pp. 6–10; §3, pp. 10–12: The two congruences supply the algebraic and analytic trace-ideal constraints.
- [RITTER-WEISS-CONGRUENCES-II](#source-ritter-weiss-congruences-ii), Propositions 4–5 and 9; Lemma 10, pp.5–11: Proof of the analytic congruence used by RW2010 Proposition 6.

<a id="ne6-rw-logarithmic-integrality"></a>

### NE.6.33. Ritter–Weiss logarithmic integrality criterion

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-logarithmic-integrality` · theorem.

Under the odd-prime, totally real, one-dimensional Ritter–Weiss hypotheses and μ_(K/k)=0, existence of Θ∈K₁(QG) with Det Θ=L_(K/k) and ∂Θ=℧_(K/k) is equivalent to L_(K/k)∈Det K₁(Λ^G). After p-elementary reduction, this is equivalent to t_(K/k)∈T(Λ^G). The implication from integral t to a multiplicative preimage requires the torsion congruence; the logarithm alone loses torsion information. The refined class ℧ is not silently identified with −[C] without the requested arithmetic comparison.

**Hypotheses.**

- p odd; K/k totally real, k/Q finite, K/k_cyc finite; finite S contains all archimedean and ramified primes; μ_(K/k)=0.
- For the second equivalence use p-elementary G and the source’s compatible character-function space.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use Wiles’s abelian main conjecture to identify the localization boundary in the central-completion reduction.
2. Apply character induction to reduce to p-elementary G.
3. Use the integral logarithm square for the easy implication; for the converse isolate the torsion correction and kill it using the index-p torsion congruence.

**Check.**

- A torsion character function may have zero logarithm; zero logarithm alone does not put it in Det of the integral K₁.
- The μ=0 hypothesis is retained when moving the boundary problem to the central completed localization.

**Prerequisites:** [NE.6/rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure](#ne6-rw-logarithmic-pseudomeasure), [NE.6/rw-mobius-congruences](#ne6-rw-mobius-congruences), `IntegralIwasawaTheory:I.5`, `KTheoryLowDegrees:U.3`.

**Sources.**

- [RITTER-WEISS-2010](#source-ritter-weiss-2010), Propositions 1–3, pp. 2–5; §6, Lemma k, pp. 25–27: States both reductions and explains the indispensable torsion correction in the logarithmic converse.

<a id="ne6-rw-main-theorem"></a>

### NE.6.34. Ritter–Weiss main theorem with μ = 0

**Target:** `NoncommutativeAndEquivariantIwasawa:NE.6/rw-main-theorem` · theorem.

Let p be odd and K/k a totally real Galois extension with k/Q finite, K containing k_cyc and [K:k_cyc]<∞. Let S contain all archimedean and ramified places and suppose μ_(K/k)=0. There exists Θ∈K₁(QG) with Det Θ=L_(K/k,S) and ∂Θ=℧_(K/k,S). The theorem proves existence; uniqueness in K₁(QG) is not part of its conclusion. Its comparison with the canonical-localization version requires both the localization maps and the refined-module/dual-complex sign comparison. Equivalently, in the source’s μ=0 criterion, L_(K/k,S) lies in Det K₁(B_G), where B_G=Λ∧G is the completed central-regular localization. This completed preimage, rather than the class ℧, is the input of completed-zeta-canonical-descent.

**Hypotheses.**

- Exactly the odd-prime, finite-over-cyclotomic, totally real and μ=0 hypotheses in the statement.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the logarithmic integrality criterion and the p-elementary reduction.
2. Use the Möbius congruences and Theorem 7 to lift integral logarithmic pseudomeasures through a central subgroup of exponent p.
3. Induct on the order of the commutator subgroup and the index of the center. Lemma h detects additive classes by all abelianized restrictions.
4. Choose a lift with support of minimal size. For its minimal U, restriction to all maximal proper V forces the coefficients to vanish unless U/A is cyclic and the supported coset generates U/A. Lemma g expresses the remaining trace coefficients in a basis; Claim 3 lifts them to τ_G(y(c−1)). This correction preserves deflation modulo C and restriction to A, removes U, and only affects groups containing a conjugate of U, which were already in the support by Claim 1.
